> Review this feedback from Co-Developer-1.  Re-evaluate your plan and incorporate what has merit in achieving 
the objectives.  Disagreement in the spirit of improvements is always welcome.  

<Co-Developer-1>
Findings

  - [BLOCK] Atomicity still underspecified: plan uses self._session.on_commit,
    which AsyncSession doesn’t expose, and action methods can’t open their own
    transactions if routes already wrap async with session.begin(). This risks
    event persistence outside the state transition transaction. apps/api/
    application/workflow_service.py, apps/api/routes/workflows.py.
  - [BLOCK] “All events persisted” isn’t actually mapped: removing emit_event()
    without a clear replacement for workflow.started, step.started,
    artifact.delta/final, step.awaiting_review leaves most events unsequenced.
    The plan doesn’t say how sync_db_from_state emits them atomically. apps/api/
    routes/workflows.py, apps/api/application/workflow_service.py.
  - [BLOCK] Replay correctness remains brittle: removing broker backlog requires
    either default from_sequence=0 or test updates; the proposed re-check logic
    also needs broker changes to avoid duplicate/backlog interleaving. apps/api/
    application/event_stream.py, apps/api/routes/workflows.py, apps/api/tests/
    e2e/test_gate_e.py.
  - [BLOCK] Staleness trigger SQL is incorrect and incomplete: it references
    from_step_number (nonexistent) and only marks older revisions of the same
    step, not downstream artifacts/links as required by §9.4/γ4. infra/db/
    migrations/versions/003_remediation.py, apps/api/infrastructure/db/models/
    traceability.py, apps/api/infrastructure/db/models/artifact.py.
  - [HIGH] state_version increment rules remain vague (new_artifacts_created/
    step_completed not defined). Needs a concrete diff of status/phase/artifact/
    staleness changes or it will miss required increments. apps/api/application/
    workflow_service.py.

    for ValidationErrorResponse (400). If you keep 422, log a deviation or add a
    custom handler. apps/api/routes/workflows.py, docs/Issued/Use-
    202601041305/4_SOLVER-technical-spec_V2_7_3.md.
  - [MEDIUM] SSE payloads must include event_type per §12.3; the proposed
    to_sse_dict() example omits it. apps/api/infrastructure/db/models/
    workflow_event.py, apps/api/tests/e2e/test_gate_e.py.
  - [MEDIUM] Adding a separate event_id column is likely redundant with
    workflow_events.id. If needed, reuse id to avoid extra migration risk.
    infra/db/migrations/versions/003_remediation.py, apps/api/infrastructure/db/
    models/workflow_event.py.

  Questions

  - Where will non-action events be persisted and sequenced—sync_db_from_state,
    graph nodes, or a dedicated event layer?
  - How will after-commit broadcasting be implemented with AsyncSession (event
    listener vs route-level publish)?

  Recommendation: REVISE
  Direction is good, but the plan still leaves atomicity, full event coverage,
  and staleness propagation unresolved. Tighten those before implementation.

     ---
     Remediation Plan

     Fix 1: Atomic Event Persistence + Full Event Coverage (Issues 1, 2, 15, 16)

     Problem:
     - session.on_commit doesn't exist on AsyncSession
     - Non-action events (workflow.started, step.started, artifact.*) have no persistence point
     - SSE payloads missing event_type
     - artifact.delta/artifact.final are SYNTHETIC events generated in routes after graph execution — removing 
     emit_event() without replacement breaks Gate E

     Solution:
     - Service persists "native" events (workflow.started, step.started, step.awaiting_review, action events) in
      transaction, RETURNS them
     - Route persists "synthetic" events (artifact.delta, artifact.final) INSIDE the same transaction
     - Route publishes ALL events AFTER transaction commits

     Changes:

     1. Transaction boundary ownership:
     # Route owns the transaction boundary
     @router.post("/workflows/{id}/approve")
     async def approve_step(...):
         events_to_publish = []

         async with db.session() as session:
             async with session.begin():  # Transaction boundary
                 service = WorkflowService(session)
                 event_repo = WorkflowEventRepository(session)

                 # 1. Action persists its own event
                 action_events = await service.approve_step(...)
                 events_to_publish.extend(action_events)

                 # 2. Run graph (inside transaction)
                 result = await graph.ainvoke(...)

                 # 3. sync_db_from_state persists native events
                 sync_events = await service.sync_db_from_state(...)
                 events_to_publish.extend(sync_events)

                 # 4. Route persists synthetic artifact events (INSIDE transaction)
                 for artifact in result.get("new_artifacts", []):
                     event = await event_repo.create_event(
                         workflow_id=workflow.id,
                         event_type="artifact.final",
                         payload={"artifact_id": str(artifact.id), ...},
                     )
                     events_to_publish.append(event)

             # Transaction committed here

         # 5. Publish ALL events AFTER transaction commits
         for event in events_to_publish:
             await broker.publish(
                 workflow.workflow_id,
                 SSEEvent(event.event_type, event.to_sse_dict(workflow.workflow_id))
             )
     2. Event type mapping (WHERE each event gets persisted):
     | Event Type           | Persisted By | Location              | Notes                      |
     |----------------------|--------------|-----------------------|----------------------------|
     | workflow.started     | Service      | create_workflow()     | Native                     |
     | step.started         | Service      | sync_db_from_state()  | Native                     |
     | step.awaiting_review | Service      | sync_db_from_state()  | Native                     |
     | step.approved        | Service      | approve_step()        | Native                     |
     | step.revised         | Service      | revise_step()         | Native                     |
     | step.clarified       | Service      | clarify_step()        | Native                     |
     | artifact.delta       | Route        | After graph chunk     | Synthetic — route persists |
     | artifact.final       | Route        | After graph completes | Synthetic — route persists |
     | workflow.completed   | Service      | sync_db_from_state()  | Native                     |

     3. Update workflow_service.py methods to return events (not manage transaction):
     async def approve_step(self, ...) -> list[WorkflowEvent]:
         """Runs INSIDE caller's transaction. Returns events to publish."""
         # NOTE: Does NOT call session.begin() — caller owns transaction

         # ... validation, state changes ...

         # Persist step.approved event
         event = await self._event_repo.create_event(
             workflow_id=workflow.id,
             event_type="step.approved",
             payload={...},
         )
         return [event]

     async def sync_db_from_state(self, ...) -> list[WorkflowEvent]:
         """Runs INSIDE caller's transaction. Persists native events."""
         events = []

         if step_started:
             events.append(await self._event_repo.create_event(..., "step.started", ...))
         if status_changed_to_awaiting_review:
             events.append(await self._event_repo.create_event(..., "step.awaiting_review", ...))

         return events  # Does NOT include synthetic artifact events
     4. Fix to_sse_dict() to include event_type and use id as event_id:
     def to_sse_dict(self, workflow_id_str: str) -> dict:
         return {
             "event_id": str(self.id),  # Use existing PK, no new column needed
             "event_type": self.event_type,  # Required per §12.3
             "sequence": self.sequence,
             "workflow_id": workflow_id_str,  # External string ID
             "timestamp": self.created_at.isoformat(),
             **self.payload,
         }
     5. For streaming artifact deltas (artifact.delta):
       - Route uses graph.astream() to get chunks
       - After each chunk, persist artifact.delta event INSIDE transaction
       - Collect events, publish ALL after transaction commits

     Key invariant: All events (native + synthetic) are persisted INSIDE the same transaction as the state 
     change, then published AFTER commit.

     CRITICAL CLARIFICATION — Graph Execution & Transaction Scope:

     The current plan runs graph.ainvoke() INSIDE the transaction. This holds the DB connection open during 
     LLM/graph execution. Two options:

     Option A (Current Plan - Atomic):
     - Run graph inside transaction
     - Pro: Full atomicity — state change + all events in single transaction
     - Con: Holds DB connection during LLM execution (potentially seconds)
     - Mitigation: Use connection pool with sufficient connections; set statement timeout

     Option B (Alternative - Non-Atomic Synthetic):
     - Run graph OUTSIDE transaction
     - Native events atomic; synthetic events persisted in separate transaction after graph
     - Pro: Short transaction duration
     - Con: Synthetic events not atomic with state change (could be orphaned if crash after graph but before 
     persist)

     Decision during implementation: Evaluate current route structure. If graph already runs outside transaction
      today, Option B may be safer. If it runs inside, Option A preserves existing behavior.

     Files:
     - apps/api/application/workflow_service.py (return events, no internal transaction)
     - apps/api/routes/workflows.py (own transaction, persist synthetic events, publish after commit)
     - apps/api/infrastructure/db/models/workflow_event.py (fix to_sse_dict)

     ---
     Fix 2: SSE Replay Correctness (Issue 3)

     Problem: Current subscribe() always replays backlog first, which mixes with DB replay causing 
     duplicates/gaps.

     Solution: Add subscribe_live() method to EventBroker. Replay uses ONLY DB events, then transitions to 
     live-only broker stream.

     Changes:

     1. Add subscribe_live() to EventBroker (event_stream.py):
     async def subscribe_live(
         self,
         workflow_id: str,
         timeout: float = 30.0,
     ) -> AsyncIterator[SSEEvent]:
         """Subscribe to LIVE events only (no backlog replay).

         Use this when replaying from DB to avoid backlog mixing.
         """
         queue: asyncio.Queue[SSEEvent] = asyncio.Queue()

         async with self._lock:
             self._queues[workflow_id] = queue
             # NO backlog replay - just register for live events

         try:
             while True:
                 try:
                     event = await asyncio.wait_for(queue.get(), timeout=timeout)
                     yield event
                 except asyncio.TimeoutError:
                     yield HEARTBEAT_EVENT
         except asyncio.CancelledError:
             raise
         finally:
             async with self._lock:
                 if self._queues.get(workflow_id) is queue:
                     del self._queues[workflow_id]
     2. Fix stream endpoint replay (routes/workflows.py):
     async def stream_events(workflow_id: str, from_sequence: int = 0):  # Default 0
         # 1. Subscribe to broker FIRST (live-only, no backlog)
         live_stream = broker.subscribe_live(workflow_id)

         # 2. Get max sequence at subscription time
         max_seq = await event_repo.get_max_sequence(workflow_id)

         # 3. Replay DB events: from_sequence < seq <= max_seq
         db_events = await event_repo.list_events(
             workflow_id, from_sequence=from_sequence, to_sequence=max_seq
         )
         for event in db_events:
             yield format_sse(event.to_sse_dict(workflow.workflow_id))

         # 4. Consume live events, dropping any with sequence <= max_seq
         async for event in live_stream:
             if event.payload.get("sequence", 0) > max_seq:
                 yield format_sse(event.payload)
     3. Add get_max_sequence() to WorkflowEventRepository:
     async def get_max_sequence(self, workflow_id: UUID) -> int:
         stmt = select(func.coalesce(func.max(WorkflowEvent.sequence), 0)).where(
             WorkflowEvent.workflow_id == workflow_id
         )
         result = await self._session.execute(stmt)
         return result.scalar_one()
     4. Update tests to pass from_sequence=0 (or update endpoint default)

     Files:
     - apps/api/application/event_stream.py (add subscribe_live)
     - apps/api/routes/workflows.py (fix replay logic)
     - apps/api/infrastructure/db/repositories/workflow_event.py (add get_max_sequence)
     - apps/api/tests/e2e/test_gate_e.py (update if needed)

     ---
     Fix 3: Required Optimistic Concurrency Fields (Issues 8, 9)

     Problem: expected_state_version is optional; expected_position doesn't exist.

     Solution: Make both fields required. Validate full position including step_name.

     Changes:

     1. Update request schemas (routes/workflows.py):
     class ExpectedPosition(BaseModel):
         """Expected workflow position for optimistic concurrency."""
         pass_type: str
         step_name: str
         step_number: int
         status: str

     class ApproveRequest(BaseModel):
         actor_id: Optional[str] = None
         expected_state_version: int = Field(..., description="Required per §11.1")
         expected_position: ExpectedPosition = Field(..., description="Required per §11.1")

     # Same for ReviseRequest, ClarifyRequest
     2. Replace _validate_state_version with _validate_optimistic_concurrency:
     def _validate_optimistic_concurrency(
         self,
         workflow: Workflow,
         step_execution: StepExecution,
         expected_state_version: int,
         expected_position: ExpectedPosition,
     ) -> None:
         # Version check
         if expected_state_version != workflow.state_version:
             raise StateVersionMismatchError(
                 expected=expected_state_version,
                 actual=workflow.state_version,
             )

         # Full position check (includes step_name)
         if (expected_position.pass_type != workflow.current_pass.value or
             expected_position.step_name != workflow.current_step.value or
             expected_position.step_number != workflow.current_step_number or
             expected_position.status != step_execution.status.value):
             raise StateVersionMismatchError(
                 message="Position mismatch",
                 expected=expected_position.model_dump(),
                 actual={...},
             )
     3. Remove the if expected_state_version is None: return bypass (line 319-320)

     Files:

     - apps/api/application/workflow_service.py (validation + signatures)

     ---
     Fix 4: Staleness Schema + Trigger Alignment (Issues 4, 5, 6, 7, 17)

     Problem:
     - Artifact model has is_stale, spec requires stale
     - Missing supersedes/superseded_by columns for lineage
     - Trigger uses nonexistent from_step_number (column is from_step)
     - Trigger only marks same-step older revisions, not DOWNSTREAM artifacts per §9.4

     Solution: Align schema with spec. Use exact trigger SQL from spec §7.

     Changes:

     1. Create NEW migration 003_remediation.py:
     -- ============================================================
     -- ARTIFACT LINEAGE COLUMNS (per spec §7)
     -- ============================================================
     ALTER TABLE artifacts
     ADD COLUMN supersedes UUID REFERENCES artifacts(id),
     ADD COLUMN superseded_by UUID REFERENCES artifacts(id);

     -- Rename is_stale to stale (spec uses 'stale')
     ALTER TABLE artifacts RENAME COLUMN is_stale TO stale;

     -- Unique index: exactly one "latest" per lineage (methodology_doc)
     CREATE UNIQUE INDEX idx_latest_methodology_doc ON artifacts(
         workflow_id, step_name, artifact_type, document_type, document_version
     ) WHERE artifact_type = 'methodology_doc' AND superseded_by IS NULL;

     -- Unique index: exactly one "latest" per lineage (step_package)
     CREATE UNIQUE INDEX idx_latest_step_package ON artifacts(
         workflow_id, step_name, artifact_type
     ) WHERE artifact_type = 'step_package' AND superseded_by IS NULL;

     -- Convenience view for latest artifacts
     CREATE VIEW latest_artifacts AS
     SELECT * FROM artifacts WHERE superseded_by IS NULL;

     -- ============================================================
     -- TRACEABILITY LINK STALENESS (per spec §7)
     -- ============================================================
     ALTER TABLE traceability_links
     ADD COLUMN stale BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,
     ADD COLUMN stale_reason VARCHAR(500) NULL,
     ADD COLUMN stale_since TIMESTAMPTZ NULL,
     ADD COLUMN validated_at TIMESTAMPTZ NULL;

     -- ============================================================
     -- STALENESS PROPAGATION TRIGGER (exact spec §7 SQL)
     -- ============================================================
     -- When a new artifact revision is inserted (supersedes IS NOT NULL),
     -- mark DOWNSTREAM latest artifacts and traceability links as stale.

     CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION propagate_staleness()
     RETURNS TRIGGER AS $$
     BEGIN
         -- Only propagate if this is a new revision (not first creation)
         IF NEW.supersedes IS NOT NULL THEN
             -- Mark downstream latest artifacts as stale
             UPDATE artifacts
             SET stale = TRUE,
                 stale_reason = 'upstream_step_' || NEW.step_number || '_revised',
                 stale_since = NOW()
             WHERE workflow_id = NEW.workflow_id
               AND step_number > NEW.step_number  -- DOWNSTREAM steps
               AND superseded_by IS NULL  -- Only mark latest versions
               AND stale = FALSE;

             -- Also mark affected traceability links
             UPDATE traceability_links
             SET stale = TRUE,
                 stale_reason = 'source_step_' || NEW.step_number || '_revised',
                 stale_since = NOW()
             WHERE workflow_id = NEW.workflow_id
               AND from_step = NEW.step_number  -- Links FROM this step
               AND stale = FALSE;
         END IF;
         RETURN NEW;
     END;
     $$ LANGUAGE plpgsql;

     -- Trigger fires on INSERT when supersedes IS NOT NULL (new revision)
     CREATE TRIGGER artifact_staleness_trigger
     AFTER INSERT ON artifacts
     FOR EACH ROW
     WHEN (NEW.supersedes IS NOT NULL)
     EXECUTE FUNCTION propagate_staleness();

     -- ============================================================
     -- ADVISORY LOCK FIX (collision-resistant)
     -- ============================================================
     CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION get_next_event_sequence(p_workflow_id UUID)
     RETURNS INTEGER AS $$
     DECLARE
         next_seq INTEGER;
         lock_id BIGINT;
     BEGIN
         lock_id := hashtext(p_workflow_id::text)::bigint;
         PERFORM pg_advisory_xact_lock(lock_id);
         SELECT COALESCE(MAX(sequence), 0) + 1 INTO next_seq
         FROM workflow_events WHERE workflow_id = p_workflow_id;
         RETURN next_seq;
     END;
     $$ LANGUAGE plpgsql;

     -- ============================================================
     -- STEP EXECUTION UPDATED_AT
     -- ============================================================
     ALTER TABLE step_executions
     ADD COLUMN updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW();

     CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION update_step_execution_timestamp()
     RETURNS TRIGGER AS $$
     BEGIN
         NEW.updated_at = NOW();
         RETURN NEW;
     END;
     $$ LANGUAGE plpgsql;

     CREATE TRIGGER step_execution_updated_at
     BEFORE UPDATE ON step_executions
     FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION update_step_execution_timestamp();

     Fix 6: Validation Error Handling (Issue 14)

     Problem: Spec requires ValidationErrorResponse (400) for missing fields; FastAPI returns 422.

     Solution: Add custom exception handler that applies to ALL request validation errors across ALL endpoints.

     Changes:

     1. Add global exception handler in main.py:
     from fastapi.exceptions import RequestValidationError
     from starlette.responses import JSONResponse

     class ValidationErrorResponse(BaseModel):
         """400 response for request validation failures."""
         error_code: str = "VALIDATION_ERROR"
         message: str
         errors: list[dict]

     @app.exception_handler(RequestValidationError)
     async def validation_exception_handler(request: Request, exc: RequestValidationError):
         """Convert FastAPI 422 to spec-compliant 400 ValidationErrorResponse.

         Applies to ALL endpoints, not just action endpoints.
         """
         return JSONResponse(
             status_code=400,
             content=ValidationErrorResponse(
                 message="Request validation failed",
                 errors=[{"loc": list(e["loc"]), "msg": e["msg"], "type": e["type"]} for e in exc.errors()],
             ).model_dump(),
         )

     Note: This handler applies globally to all endpoints (workflows, messages, progress, etc.), ensuring spec 
     compliance throughout.

     Fix 7: state_version Increment Rule (Issue 13)

     Problem: state_version rules vague; need concrete before/after comparison. Must include status/phase 
     changes EVEN when position doesn't change.

     Solution: Use explicit before/after comparisons. Increment on ANY state-eligibility change.

     Changes:

     1. Update sync_db_from_state (workflow_service.py):
     # Capture state BEFORE changes
     old_pass = workflow.current_pass
     old_step = workflow.current_step
     old_step_number = workflow.current_step_number
     old_status = step_execution.status if step_execution else None
     old_phase = step_execution.phase if step_execution else None

     # ... apply state changes ...

     # Detect state-eligibility changes (Contract §11.3)
     # IMPORTANT: status/phase changes increment EVEN if position unchanged
     state_eligibility_changed = (
         # Position changes
         old_pass != workflow.current_pass or
         old_step != workflow.current_step or
         old_step_number != workflow.current_step_number or
         # Status changes (EVEN without position change)
         (step_execution and old_status != step_execution.status) or
         # Phase changes (EVEN without position change)
         (step_execution and old_phase != step_execution.phase) or
         # New artifacts created
         len(artifact_results) > 0
     )

     if state_eligibility_changed:
         await self._increment_state_version(workflow)
     2. Action methods always increment (they change status/phase):
       - approve_step(): status changes (awaiting_review → approved) → increment
       - revise_step(): status changes + new artifact → increment
       - clarify_step(): phase changes → increment
     3. Explicit increment scenarios:
     | Change                             | Position Changed? | Increment? |
     |------------------------------------|-------------------|------------|
     | Step advances                      | Yes               | Yes        |
     | Status: pending → running          | No                | Yes        |
     | Status: awaiting_review → approved | No                | Yes        |
     | Phase: received → drafting         | No                | Yes        |
     | New artifact created               | No                | Yes        |
     | Staleness updated                  | No                | Yes        |

     ---
     Success Criteria

     Event Persistence + Atomicity

     - Route owns transaction boundary (async with session.begin())
     - Service methods run INSIDE caller's transaction, return events list
     - Native events (workflow.started, step.*, action events) persisted by service
     - Synthetic events (artifact.delta, artifact.final) persisted by route INSIDE same transaction
     - ALL events published AFTER transaction commits (route does publish loop)
     - SSE payloads include event_type, event_id (=workflow_events.id), sequence, external workflow_id

     SSE Replay

     - subscribe_live() method exists for live-only streaming (no backlog)
     - Replay: subscribe FIRST, get max_seq, replay DB, drop broker events <= max_seq

     Optimistic Concurrency

     - expected_state_version and expected_position (with step_name) REQUIRED
     - _validate_optimistic_concurrency validates full position (pass_type, step_name, step_number, status)
     - No None bypass in validation

     Staleness Schema (spec §7 alignment)

     - Artifact model has supersedes, superseded_by, stale (not is_stale)
     - Unique indexes enforce exactly one "latest" per artifact lineage
     - latest_artifacts view exists
     - Staleness trigger fires on INSERT when supersedes IS NOT NULL
     - Staleness propagates to DOWNSTREAM artifacts (step_number > NEW.step_number)
     - Traceability links have stale, stale_reason, stale_since, validated_at
     - StalenessResponse includes position, blocking_reasons, stale_links
     - acknowledge-stale and re-execute endpoints exist

     Other

     - Progress endpoint has real artifact revision, real updated_at
     - Advisory lock uses hashtext() — no collision risk
     - Validation errors return 400 globally (not FastAPI's 422) — applies to ALL endpoints
     - state_version increments on status/phase changes EVEN when position unchanged
     - New migration 003 created (002 unchanged)
     - All existing tests pass (including Gate E)

     Guardrails (HIGH priority)

     - stale column replaces is_stale EVERYWHERE — models, queries, progress endpoint, staleness endpoint (avoid
      split-brain staleness)
     - Gate E tests updated — if they assume backlog replay without from_sequence, update to pass 
     from_sequence=0 (or set endpoint default to 0)

      861 +            (old_phase is not None and old_phase != new_phase) or                                 
      862 +            # New step execution created (old_status was None)                                    
      863 +            old_status is None or                                                                 
      864 +            # New artifacts created                                                               
      865 +            len(artifact_results) > 0                                                             
      866 +        )                                                                                         
      867 +                                                                                                  
      868 +        if state_eligibility_changed:                                                             
      869 +            await self._increment_state_version(workflow)                                         
      870 +                                                                                                  
      871          await self._session.flush()
      872

      152 +

      118 +    async def subscribe_live(                                                                     
      119 +        self,                                                                                     
      120 +        workflow_id: str,                                                                         
      121 +        timeout: float = 30.0,                                                                    
      122 +    ) -> AsyncIterator[SSEEvent]:                                                                 
      123 +        """Subscribe to LIVE events only (no backlog replay).                                     
      124 +                                                                                                  
      125 +        Use this when replaying from DB to avoid backlog mixing.                                  
      126 +        Per Fix 2 in remediation plan: subscribe_live for race-free replay.                       
      127 +                                                                                                  
      128 +        Args:                                                                                     
      129 +            workflow_id: Workflow to subscribe to.                                                
      130 +            timeout: Max seconds to wait for next event (default 30s).                            
      131 +                                                                                                  
      132 +        Yields:                                                                                   
      133 +            SSEEvent objects from live stream only (no backlog).                                  
      134 +        """                                                                                       
      135 +        queue: asyncio.Queue[SSEEvent] = asyncio.Queue()                                          
      136 +                                                                                                  
      137 +        async with self._lock:                                                                    
      138 +            # Register subscriber - NO backlog replay                                             
      139 +            self._queues[workflow_id] = queue                                                     
      140 +                                                                                                  
      141 +        # Stream live events with timeout (no backlog replay)                                     
      142 +        try:                                                                                      
      143 +            while True:                                                                           
      144 +                try:                                                                              
      145 +                    event = await asyncio.wait_for(queue.get(), timeout=timeout)                  
      146 +                    yield event                                                                   
      147 +                except asyncio.TimeoutError:                                                      
      148 +                    # Yield heartbeat to keep connection alive                                    
      149 +                    yield HEARTBEAT_EVENT                                                         
      150 +        except asyncio.CancelledError:                                                            
      151 +            # Clean exit on cancellation                                                          
      152 +            raise                                                                                 
      153 +        finally:                                                                                  
      154 +            # Cleanup on disconnect                                                               
      155 +            async with self._lock:                                                                
      156 +                if self._queues.get(workflow_id) is queue:                                        
      157 +                    del self._queues[workflow_id]                                                 
      158 +

      160          """Clear backlog for a workflow.
      161

     ---
     Remediation Plan

     Fix 1: Atomic Event Persistence + Full Event Coverage (Issues 1, 2, 15, 16)

     Problem:
     - session.on_commit doesn't exist on AsyncSession
     - Non-action events (workflow.started, step.started, artifact.*) have no persistence point
     - SSE payloads missing event_type
     - artifact.delta/artifact.final are SYNTHETIC events generated in routes after graph execution — removing 
     emit_event() without replacement breaks Gate E

     Solution:
     - Service persists "native" events (workflow.started, step.started, step.awaiting_review, action events) in
      transaction, RETURNS them
     - Route persists "synthetic" events (artifact.delta, artifact.final) INSIDE the same transaction
     - Route publishes ALL events AFTER transaction commits

     Changes:

     1. Transaction boundary ownership:
     # Route owns the transaction boundary
     @router.post("/workflows/{id}/approve")
     async def approve_step(...):
         events_to_publish = []

         async with db.session() as session:
             async with session.begin():  # Transaction boundary
                 service = WorkflowService(session)
                 event_repo = WorkflowEventRepository(session)

                 # 1. Action persists its own event
                 action_events = await service.approve_step(...)
                 events_to_publish.extend(action_events)

                 # 2. Run graph (inside transaction)
                 result = await graph.ainvoke(...)

                 # 3. sync_db_from_state persists native events
                 sync_events = await service.sync_db_from_state(...)
                 events_to_publish.extend(sync_events)

                 # 4. Route persists synthetic artifact events (INSIDE transaction)
                 for artifact in result.get("new_artifacts", []):
                     event = await event_repo.create_event(
                         workflow_id=workflow.id,
                         event_type="artifact.final",
                         payload={"artifact_id": str(artifact.id), ...},
                     )
                     events_to_publish.append(event)

             # Transaction committed here

         # 5. Publish ALL events AFTER transaction commits
         for event in events_to_publish:
             await broker.publish(
                 workflow.workflow_id,
                 SSEEvent(event.event_type, event.to_sse_dict(workflow.workflow_id))
             )
     2. Event type mapping (WHERE each event gets persisted):
     | Event Type           | Persisted By | Location              | Notes                      |
     |----------------------|--------------|-----------------------|----------------------------|
     | workflow.started     | Service      | create_workflow()     | Native                     |
     | step.started         | Service      | sync_db_from_state()  | Native                     |
     | step.awaiting_review | Service      | sync_db_from_state()  | Native                     |
     | step.approved        | Service      | approve_step()        | Native                     |
     | step.revised         | Service      | revise_step()         | Native                     |
     | step.clarified       | Service      | clarify_step()        | Native                     |
     | artifact.delta       | Route        | After graph chunk     | Synthetic — route persists |
     | artifact.final       | Route        | After graph completes | Synthetic — route persists |
     | workflow.completed   | Service      | sync_db_from_state()  | Native                     |

     3. Update workflow_service.py methods to return events (not manage transaction):
     async def approve_step(self, ...) -> list[WorkflowEvent]:
         """Runs INSIDE caller's transaction. Returns events to publish."""
         # NOTE: Does NOT call session.begin() — caller owns transaction

         # ... validation, state changes ...

         # Persist step.approved event
         event = await self._event_repo.create_event(
             workflow_id=workflow.id,
             event_type="step.approved",
             payload={...},
         )
         return [event]

     async def sync_db_from_state(self, ...) -> list[WorkflowEvent]:
         """Runs INSIDE caller's transaction. Persists native events."""
         events = []

         if step_started:
             events.append(await self._event_repo.create_event(..., "step.started", ...))
         if status_changed_to_awaiting_review:
             events.append(await self._event_repo.create_event(..., "step.awaiting_review", ...))

         return events  # Does NOT include synthetic artifact events
     4. Fix to_sse_dict() to include event_type and use id as event_id:
     def to_sse_dict(self, workflow_id_str: str) -> dict:
         return {
             "event_id": str(self.id),  # Use existing PK, no new column needed
             "event_type": self.event_type,  # Required per §12.3
             "sequence": self.sequence,
             "workflow_id": workflow_id_str,  # External string ID
             "timestamp": self.created_at.isoformat(),
             **self.payload,
         }
     5. For streaming artifact deltas (artifact.delta):
       - Route uses graph.astream() to get chunks
       - After each chunk, persist artifact.delta event INSIDE transaction
       - Collect events, publish ALL after transaction commits

     Key invariant: All events (native + synthetic) are persisted INSIDE the same transaction as the state 
     change, then published AFTER commit.

     CRITICAL CLARIFICATION — Graph Execution & Transaction Scope:

     The current plan runs graph.ainvoke() INSIDE the transaction. This holds the DB connection open during 
     LLM/graph execution. Two options:

     Option A (Current Plan - Atomic):
     - Run graph inside transaction
     - Pro: Full atomicity — state change + all events in single transaction
     - Con: Holds DB connection during LLM execution (potentially seconds)
     - Mitigation: Use connection pool with sufficient connections; set statement timeout

     Option B (Alternative - Non-Atomic Synthetic):
     - Run graph OUTSIDE transaction
     - Native events atomic; synthetic events persisted in separate transaction after graph
     - Pro: Short transaction duration
     - Con: Synthetic events not atomic with state change (could be orphaned if crash after graph but before 
     persist)

     Decision during implementation: Evaluate current route structure. If graph already runs outside transaction
      today, Option B may be safer. If it runs inside, Option A preserves existing behavior.

     Files:
     - apps/api/application/workflow_service.py (return events, no internal transaction)
     - apps/api/routes/workflows.py (own transaction, persist synthetic events, publish after commit)
     - apps/api/infrastructure/db/models/workflow_event.py (fix to_sse_dict)

     ---
     Fix 2: SSE Replay Correctness (Issue 3)

     Problem: Current subscribe() always replays backlog first, which mixes with DB replay causing 
     duplicates/gaps.

     Solution: Add subscribe_live() method to EventBroker. Replay uses ONLY DB events, then transitions to 
     live-only broker stream.

     Changes:

     1. Add subscribe_live() to EventBroker (event_stream.py):
     async def subscribe_live(
         self,
         workflow_id: str,
         timeout: float = 30.0,
     ) -> AsyncIterator[SSEEvent]:
         """Subscribe to LIVE events only (no backlog replay).

         Use this when replaying from DB to avoid backlog mixing.
         """
         queue: asyncio.Queue[SSEEvent] = asyncio.Queue()

         async with self._lock:
             self._queues[workflow_id] = queue
             # NO backlog replay - just register for live events

         try:
             while True:
                 try:
                     event = await asyncio.wait_for(queue.get(), timeout=timeout)
                     yield event
                 except asyncio.TimeoutError:
                     yield HEARTBEAT_EVENT
         except asyncio.CancelledError:
             raise
         finally:
             async with self._lock:
                 if self._queues.get(workflow_id) is queue:
                     del self._queues[workflow_id]
     2. Fix stream endpoint replay (routes/workflows.py):
     async def stream_events(workflow_id: str, from_sequence: int = 0):  # Default 0
         # 1. Subscribe to broker FIRST (live-only, no backlog)
         live_stream = broker.subscribe_live(workflow_id)

         # 2. Get max sequence at subscription time
         max_seq = await event_repo.get_max_sequence(workflow_id)

         # 3. Replay DB events: from_sequence < seq <= max_seq
         db_events = await event_repo.list_events(
             workflow_id, from_sequence=from_sequence, to_sequence=max_seq
         )
         for event in db_events:
             yield format_sse(event.to_sse_dict(workflow.workflow_id))

         # 4. Consume live events, dropping any with sequence <= max_seq
         async for event in live_stream:
             if event.payload.get("sequence", 0) > max_seq:
                 yield format_sse(event.payload)
     3. Add get_max_sequence() to WorkflowEventRepository:
     async def get_max_sequence(self, workflow_id: UUID) -> int:
         stmt = select(func.coalesce(func.max(WorkflowEvent.sequence), 0)).where(
             WorkflowEvent.workflow_id == workflow_id
         )
         result = await self._session.execute(stmt)
         return result.scalar_one()
     4. Update tests to pass from_sequence=0 (or update endpoint default)

     Files:
     - apps/api/application/event_stream.py (add subscribe_live)
     - apps/api/routes/workflows.py (fix replay logic)
     - apps/api/infrastructure/db/repositories/workflow_event.py (add get_max_sequence)
     - apps/api/tests/e2e/test_gate_e.py (update if needed)

     Fix 3: Required Optimistic Concurrency Fields (Issues 8, 9)

     Problem: expected_state_version is optional; expected_position doesn't exist.

     Solution: Make both fields required. Validate full position including step_name.

     Changes:

     1. Update request schemas (routes/workflows.py):
     class ExpectedPosition(BaseModel):
         """Expected workflow position for optimistic concurrency."""
         pass_type: str
         step_name: str
         step_number: int
         status: str

     class ApproveRequest(BaseModel):
         actor_id: Optional[str] = None
         expected_state_version: int = Field(..., description="Required per §11.1")
         expected_position: ExpectedPosition = Field(..., description="Required per §11.1")

     # Same for ReviseRequest, ClarifyRequest
     2. Replace _validate_state_version with _validate_optimistic_concurrency:
     def _validate_optimistic_concurrency(
         self,
         workflow: Workflow,
         step_execution: StepExecution,
         expected_state_version: int,
         expected_position: ExpectedPosition,
     ) -> None:
         # Version check
         if expected_state_version != workflow.state_version:
             raise StateVersionMismatchError(
                 expected=expected_state_version,
                 actual=workflow.state_version,
             )

         # Full position check (includes step_name)
         if (expected_position.pass_type != workflow.current_pass.value or
             expected_position.step_name != workflow.current_step.value or
             expected_position.step_number != workflow.current_step_number or
             expected_position.status != step_execution.status.value):
             raise StateVersionMismatchError(
                 message="Position mismatch",
                 expected=expected_position.model_dump(),
                 actual={...},
             )
     3. Remove the if expected_state_version is None: return bypass (line 319-320)

     Fix 4: Staleness Schema + Trigger Alignment (Issues 4, 5, 6, 7, 17)

     Problem:
     - Artifact model has is_stale, spec requires stale
     - Missing supersedes/superseded_by columns for lineage
     - Trigger uses nonexistent from_step_number (column is from_step)
     - Trigger only marks same-step older revisions, not DOWNSTREAM artifacts per §9.4

     Solution: Align schema with spec. Use exact trigger SQL from spec §7.

     Changes:

     1. Create NEW migration 003_remediation.py:
     -- ============================================================
     -- ARTIFACT LINEAGE COLUMNS (per spec §7)
     -- ============================================================
     ALTER TABLE artifacts
     ADD COLUMN supersedes UUID REFERENCES artifacts(id),
     ADD COLUMN superseded_by UUID REFERENCES artifacts(id);

     -- Rename is_stale to stale (spec uses 'stale')
     ALTER TABLE artifacts RENAME COLUMN is_stale TO stale;

     -- Unique index: exactly one "latest" per lineage (methodology_doc)
     CREATE UNIQUE INDEX idx_latest_methodology_doc ON artifacts(
         workflow_id, step_name, artifact_type, document_type, document_version
     ) WHERE artifact_type = 'methodology_doc' AND superseded_by IS NULL;

     -- Unique index: exactly one "latest" per lineage (step_package)
     CREATE UNIQUE INDEX idx_latest_step_package ON artifacts(
         workflow_id, step_name, artifact_type
     ) WHERE artifact_type = 'step_package' AND superseded_by IS NULL;

     -- Convenience view for latest artifacts
     CREATE VIEW latest_artifacts AS
     SELECT * FROM artifacts WHERE superseded_by IS NULL;

     -- ============================================================
     -- TRACEABILITY LINK STALENESS (per spec §7)
     -- ============================================================
     ALTER TABLE traceability_links
     ADD COLUMN stale BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,
     ADD COLUMN stale_reason VARCHAR(500) NULL,
     ADD COLUMN stale_since TIMESTAMPTZ NULL,
     ADD COLUMN validated_at TIMESTAMPTZ NULL;

     -- ============================================================
     -- STALENESS PROPAGATION TRIGGER (exact spec §7 SQL)
     -- ============================================================
     -- When a new artifact revision is inserted (supersedes IS NOT NULL),
     -- mark DOWNSTREAM latest artifacts and traceability links as stale.

     CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION propagate_staleness()
     RETURNS TRIGGER AS $$
     BEGIN
         -- Only propagate if this is a new revision (not first creation)
         IF NEW.supersedes IS NOT NULL THEN
             -- Mark downstream latest artifacts as stale
             UPDATE artifacts
             SET stale = TRUE,
                 stale_reason = 'upstream_step_' || NEW.step_number || '_revised',
                 stale_since = NOW()
             WHERE workflow_id = NEW.workflow_id
               AND step_number > NEW.step_number  -- DOWNSTREAM steps
               AND superseded_by IS NULL  -- Only mark latest versions
               AND stale = FALSE;

             -- Also mark affected traceability links
             UPDATE traceability_links
             SET stale = TRUE,
                 stale_reason = 'source_step_' || NEW.step_number || '_revised',
                 stale_since = NOW()
             WHERE workflow_id = NEW.workflow_id
               AND from_step = NEW.step_number  -- Links FROM this step
               AND stale = FALSE;
         END IF;
         RETURN NEW;
     END;
     $$ LANGUAGE plpgsql;

     -- Trigger fires on INSERT when supersedes IS NOT NULL (new revision)
     CREATE TRIGGER artifact_staleness_trigger
     AFTER INSERT ON artifacts
     FOR EACH ROW
     WHEN (NEW.supersedes IS NOT NULL)
     EXECUTE FUNCTION propagate_staleness();

     -- ============================================================
     -- ADVISORY LOCK FIX (collision-resistant)
     -- ============================================================
     CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION get_next_event_sequence(p_workflow_id UUID)
     RETURNS INTEGER AS $$
     DECLARE
         next_seq INTEGER;
         lock_id BIGINT;
     BEGIN
         lock_id := hashtext(p_workflow_id::text)::bigint;
         PERFORM pg_advisory_xact_lock(lock_id);
         SELECT COALESCE(MAX(sequence), 0) + 1 INTO next_seq
         FROM workflow_events WHERE workflow_id = p_workflow_id;
         RETURN next_seq;
     END;
     $$ LANGUAGE plpgsql;

     -- ============================================================
     -- STEP EXECUTION UPDATED_AT
     -- ============================================================
     ALTER TABLE step_executions
     ADD COLUMN updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW();

     CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION update_step_execution_timestamp()
     RETURNS TRIGGER AS $$
     BEGIN
         NEW.updated_at = NOW();
         RETURN NEW;
     END;
     $$ LANGUAGE plpgsql;

     CREATE TRIGGER step_execution_updated_at
     BEFORE UPDATE ON step_executions
     FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION update_step_execution_timestamp();
     2. Update Artifact model (artifact.py):
       - Rename is_stale → stale
       - Add supersedes, superseded_by columns
     3. Update TraceabilityLink model (traceability.py):
       - Add stale, stale_reason, stale_since, validated_at columns
     4. Update StalenessResponse schema (routes/workflows.py):
     class StalenessResponse(BaseModel):
         workflow_id: str
         position: PositionResponse
         can_complete: bool
         blocking_reasons: list[str]
         stale_artifacts: list[StaleArtifactEntry]
         stale_links: list[StaleLinkEntry]
     5. Add acknowledge-stale and re-execute endpoints per §10.2

     ---
     Fix 6: Validation Error Handling (Issue 14)

     Problem: Spec requires ValidationErrorResponse (400) for missing fields; FastAPI returns 422.

     Solution: Add custom exception handler that applies to ALL request validation errors across ALL endpoints.

     Changes:

     1. Add global exception handler in main.py:
     from fastapi.exceptions import RequestValidationError
     from starlette.responses import JSONResponse

     class ValidationErrorResponse(BaseModel):
         """400 response for request validation failures."""
         error_code: str = "VALIDATION_ERROR"
         message: str
         errors: list[dict]

     @app.exception_handler(RequestValidationError)
     async def validation_exception_handler(request: Request, exc: RequestValidationError):
         """Convert FastAPI 422 to spec-compliant 400 ValidationErrorResponse.

         Applies to ALL endpoints, not just action endpoints.
         """
         return JSONResponse(
             status_code=400,
             content=ValidationErrorResponse(
                 message="Request validation failed",
                 errors=[{"loc": list(e["loc"]), "msg": e["msg"], "type": e["type"]} for e in exc.errors()],
             ).model_dump(),
         )

     Note: This handler applies globally to all endpoints (workflows, messages, progress, etc.), ensuring spec 
     compliance throughout.

     Files:
     - apps/api/main.py (add global exception handler + schema)

     ---
     Fix 7: state_version Increment Rule (Issue 13)

     Problem: state_version rules vague; need concrete before/after comparison. Must include status/phase 
     changes EVEN when position doesn't change.

     Solution: Use explicit before/after comparisons. Increment on ANY state-eligibility change.

     Changes:

     1. Update sync_db_from_state (workflow_service.py):
     # Capture state BEFORE changes
     old_pass = workflow.current_pass
     old_step = workflow.current_step
     old_step_number = workflow.current_step_number
     old_status = step_execution.status if step_execution else None
     old_phase = step_execution.phase if step_execution else None

     # ... apply state changes ...

     # Detect state-eligibility changes (Contract §11.3)
     # IMPORTANT: status/phase changes increment EVEN if position unchanged
     state_eligibility_changed = (
         # Position changes
         old_pass != workflow.current_pass or
         old_step != workflow.current_step or
         old_step_number != workflow.current_step_number or
         # Status changes (EVEN without position change)
         (step_execution and old_status != step_execution.status) or
         # Phase changes (EVEN without position change)
         (step_execution and old_phase != step_execution.phase) or
         # New artifacts created
         len(artifact_results) > 0
     )

     if state_eligibility_changed:
         await self._increment_state_version(workflow)
     2. Action methods always increment (they change status/phase):
       - approve_step(): status changes (awaiting_review → approved) → increment
       - revise_step(): status changes + new artifact → increment
       - clarify_step(): phase changes → increment
     3. Explicit increment scenarios:
     | Change                             | Position Changed? | Increment? |
     |------------------------------------|-------------------|------------|
     | Step advances                      | Yes               | Yes        |
     | Status: pending → running          | No                | Yes        |
     | Status: awaiting_review → approved | No                | Yes        |
     | Phase: received → drafting         | No                | Yes        |
     | New artifact created               | No                | Yes        |
     | Staleness updated                  | No                | Yes        |


     Files:
     - apps/api/application/workflow_service.py

     ---
     Success Criteria

     Event Persistence + Atomicity

     - Route owns transaction boundary (async with session.begin())
     - Service methods run INSIDE caller's transaction, return events list
     - Native events (workflow.started, step.*, action events) persisted by service
     - Synthetic events (artifact.delta, artifact.final) persisted by route INSIDE same transaction
     - ALL events published AFTER transaction commits (route does publish loop)
     - SSE payloads include event_type, event_id (=workflow_events.id), sequence, external workflow_id

     SSE Replay

     - subscribe_live() method exists for live-only streaming (no backlog)
     - Replay: subscribe FIRST, get max_seq, replay DB, drop broker events <= max_seq

     Optimistic Concurrency

     - expected_state_version and expected_position (with step_name) REQUIRED
     - _validate_optimistic_concurrency validates full position (pass_type, step_name, step_number, status)
     - No None bypass in validation

     Staleness Schema (spec §7 alignment)

     - Artifact model has supersedes, superseded_by, stale (not is_stale)
     - Unique indexes enforce exactly one "latest" per artifact lineage
     - latest_artifacts view exists
     - Staleness trigger fires on INSERT when supersedes IS NOT NULL
     - Staleness propagates to DOWNSTREAM artifacts (step_number > NEW.step_number)
     - Traceability links have stale, stale_reason, stale_since, validated_at
     - StalenessResponse includes position, blocking_reasons, stale_links
     - acknowledge-stale and re-execute endpoints exist

     Other

     - Progress endpoint has real artifact revision, real updated_at
     - Advisory lock uses hashtext() — no collision risk
     - Validation errors return 400 globally (not FastAPI's 422) — applies to ALL endpoints
     - state_version increments on status/phase changes EVEN when position unchanged
     - New migration 003 created (002 unchanged)
     - All existing tests pass (including Gate E)

     Guardrails (HIGH priority)

     - stale column replaces is_stale EVERYWHERE — models, queries, progress endpoint, staleness endpoint (avoid
      split-brain staleness)
     - Gate E tests updated — if they assume backlog replay without from_sequence, update to pass 
     from_sequence=0 (or set endpoint default to 0)

     Backend Contract Alignment — Remediation Plan

     Project: solver-engine
     Working Directory: /Users/ryan/ai-env/projects/solver-engine

     Revision: Co-Developer-1 Remediation v3 (2026-01-04)

     ---
     Objective

     Fix all BLOCK/HIGH issues from Co-Developer-1's three reviews. Address critical gaps in:
     - SSE event persistence atomicity
     - Full event coverage for all event types
     - Staleness schema alignment with spec
     - Replay correctness without race conditions
     - Optimistic concurrency validation

     ---
     Issues to Fix (All Reviews Combined)

     BLOCK Issues

     | ID  | Issue                                                                     | Root Cause             
                         |
     |-----|---------------------------------------------------------------------------|------------------------
     --------------------|
     | 1   | session.on_commit doesn't exist on AsyncSession                           | Plan used non-existent 
     API                 |
     | 2   | Non-action events (workflow.started, step.started, artifact.*) not mapped | No persistence point 
     defined               |
     | 3   | Replay mixes DB events with broker backlog                                | subscribe() always 
     replays backlog         |
     | 4   | Staleness trigger SQL references from_step_number (nonexistent)           | Column is from_step    
                         |
     | 5   | Staleness only marks same-step older revisions, not downstream            | Spec requires marking 
     downstream artifacts |
     | 6   | Artifact model has is_stale, spec requires stale                          | Column name mismatch   
                         |
     | 7   | Missing supersedes/superseded_by columns on artifacts                     | Required for lineage 
     tracking              |
     | 8   | expected_state_version optional (bypasses on None)                        | Line 319-320 returns 
     early                 |
     | 9   | expected_position field completely missing                                | No schema field exists 
                         |

     HIGH Issues

     | ID  | Issue                                 | Root Cause                                  |
     |-----|---------------------------------------|---------------------------------------------|
     | 10  | latest_artifact_revision is TODO      | Line 627 sets None                          |
     | 11  | updated_at missing on StepExecution   | Model only has started_at, completed_at     |
     | 12  | Advisory lock uses first 8 UUID chars | Collision risk                              |
     | 13  | state_version increment rules vague   | Need concrete before/after comparison       |
     | 14  | Missing-field validation returns 422  | Spec requires ValidationErrorResponse (400) |

     MEDIUM Issues

     | ID  | Issue                                   | Root Cause                 |
     |-----|-----------------------------------------|----------------------------|
     | 15  | SSE payloads missing event_type         | to_sse_dict() omits it     |
     | 16  | Separate event_id column redundant      | Can use workflow_events.id |
     | 17  | Traceability links missing validated_at | Spec requires it           |

     ---
     Remediation Plan

     Fix 1: Atomic Event Persistence + Full Event Coverage (Issues 1, 2, 15, 16)

     Problem:
     - session.on_commit doesn't exist on AsyncSession
     - Non-action events (workflow.started, step.started, artifact.*) have no persistence point
     - SSE payloads missing event_type
     - artifact.delta/artifact.final are SYNTHETIC events generated in routes after graph execution — removing 
     emit_event() without replacement breaks Gate E

     Solution:
     - Service persists "native" events (workflow.started, step.started, step.awaiting_review, action events) in
      transaction, RETURNS them
     - Route persists "synthetic" events (artifact.delta, artifact.final) INSIDE the same transaction
     - Route publishes ALL events AFTER transaction commits

     Changes:

     1. Transaction boundary ownership:
     # Route owns the transaction boundary
     @router.post("/workflows/{id}/approve")
     async def approve_step(...):
         events_to_publish = []

         async with db.session() as session:
             async with session.begin():  # Transaction boundary
                 service = WorkflowService(session)
                 event_repo = WorkflowEventRepository(session)

                 # 1. Action persists its own event
                 action_events = await service.approve_step(...)
                 events_to_publish.extend(action_events)

                 # 2. Run graph (inside transaction)
                 result = await graph.ainvoke(...)

                 # 3. sync_db_from_state persists native events
                 sync_events = await service.sync_db_from_state(...)
                 events_to_publish.extend(sync_events)

                 # 4. Route persists synthetic artifact events (INSIDE transaction)
                 for artifact in result.get("new_artifacts", []):
                     event = await event_repo.create_event(
                         workflow_id=workflow.id,
                         event_type="artifact.final",
                         payload={"artifact_id": str(artifact.id), ...},
                     )
                     events_to_publish.append(event)

             # Transaction committed here

         # 5. Publish ALL events AFTER transaction commits
         for event in events_to_publish:
             await broker.publish(
                 workflow.workflow_id,
                 SSEEvent(event.event_type, event.to_sse_dict(workflow.workflow_id))
             )
     2. Event type mapping (WHERE each event gets persisted):
     | Event Type           | Persisted By | Location              | Notes                      |
     |----------------------|--------------|-----------------------|----------------------------|
     | workflow.started     | Service      | create_workflow()     | Native                     |
     | step.started         | Service      | sync_db_from_state()  | Native                     |
     | step.awaiting_review | Service      | sync_db_from_state()  | Native                     |
     | step.approved        | Service      | approve_step()        | Native                     |
     | step.revised         | Service      | revise_step()         | Native                     |
     | step.clarified       | Service      | clarify_step()        | Native                     |
     | artifact.delta       | Route        | After graph chunk     | Synthetic — route persists |
     | artifact.final       | Route        | After graph completes | Synthetic — route persists |
     | workflow.completed   | Service      | sync_db_from_state()  | Native                     |

     3. Update workflow_service.py methods to return events (not manage transaction):
     async def approve_step(self, ...) -> list[WorkflowEvent]:
         """Runs INSIDE caller's transaction. Returns events to publish."""
         # NOTE: Does NOT call session.begin() — caller owns transaction

         # ... validation, state changes ...

         # Persist step.approved event
         event = await self._event_repo.create_event(
             workflow_id=workflow.id,
             event_type="step.approved",
             payload={...},
         )
         return [event]

     async def sync_db_from_state(self, ...) -> list[WorkflowEvent]:
         """Runs INSIDE caller's transaction. Persists native events."""
         events = []

         if step_started:
             events.append(await self._event_repo.create_event(..., "step.started", ...))
         if status_changed_to_awaiting_review:
             events.append(await self._event_repo.create_event(..., "step.awaiting_review", ...))

         return events  # Does NOT include synthetic artifact events
     4. Fix to_sse_dict() to include event_type and use id as event_id:
     def to_sse_dict(self, workflow_id_str: str) -> dict:
         return {
             "event_id": str(self.id),  # Use existing PK, no new column needed
             "event_type": self.event_type,  # Required per §12.3
             "sequence": self.sequence,
             "workflow_id": workflow_id_str,  # External string ID
             "timestamp": self.created_at.isoformat(),
             **self.payload,
         }
     5. For streaming artifact deltas (artifact.delta):
       - Route uses graph.astream() to get chunks
       - After each chunk, persist artifact.delta event INSIDE transaction
       - Collect events, publish ALL after transaction commits

     Key invariant: All events (native + synthetic) are persisted INSIDE the same transaction as the state 
     change, then published AFTER commit.

     CRITICAL CLARIFICATION — Graph Execution & Transaction Scope:

     The current plan runs graph.ainvoke() INSIDE the transaction. This holds the DB connection open during 
     LLM/graph execution. Two options:

     Option A (Current Plan - Atomic):
     - Run graph inside transaction
     - Pro: Full atomicity — state change + all events in single transaction
     - Con: Holds DB connection during LLM execution (potentially seconds)
     - Mitigation: Use connection pool with sufficient connections; set statement timeout

     Option B (Alternative - Non-Atomic Synthetic):
     - Run graph OUTSIDE transaction
     - Native events atomic; synthetic events persisted in separate transaction after graph
     - Pro: Short transaction duration
     - Con: Synthetic events not atomic with state change (could be orphaned if crash after graph but before 
     persist)

     Decision during implementation: Evaluate current route structure. If graph already runs outside transaction
      today, Option B may be safer. If it runs inside, Option A preserves existing behavior.

     Files:
     - apps/api/application/workflow_service.py (return events, no internal transaction)
     - apps/api/routes/workflows.py (own transaction, persist synthetic events, publish after commit)
     - apps/api/infrastructure/db/models/workflow_event.py (fix to_sse_dict)

     ---
     Fix 2: SSE Replay Correctness (Issue 3)

     Problem: Current subscribe() always replays backlog first, which mixes with DB replay causing 
     duplicates/gaps.

     Solution: Add subscribe_live() method to EventBroker. Replay uses ONLY DB events, then transitions to 
     live-only broker stream.

     Changes:

     1. Add subscribe_live() to EventBroker (event_stream.py):
     async def subscribe_live(
         self,
         workflow_id: str,
         timeout: float = 30.0,
     ) -> AsyncIterator[SSEEvent]:
         """Subscribe to LIVE events only (no backlog replay).

         Use this when replaying from DB to avoid backlog mixing.
         """
         queue: asyncio.Queue[SSEEvent] = asyncio.Queue()

         async with self._lock:
             self._queues[workflow_id] = queue
             # NO backlog replay - just register for live events

         try:
             while True:
                 try:
                     event = await asyncio.wait_for(queue.get(), timeout=timeout)
                     yield event
                 except asyncio.TimeoutError:
                     yield HEARTBEAT_EVENT
         except asyncio.CancelledError:
             raise
         finally:
             async with self._lock:
                 if self._queues.get(workflow_id) is queue:
                     del self._queues[workflow_id]
     2. Fix stream endpoint replay (routes/workflows.py):
     async def stream_events(workflow_id: str, from_sequence: int = 0):  # Default 0
         # 1. Subscribe to broker FIRST (live-only, no backlog)
         live_stream = broker.subscribe_live(workflow_id)

         # 2. Get max sequence at subscription time
         max_seq = await event_repo.get_max_sequence(workflow_id)

         # 3. Replay DB events: from_sequence < seq <= max_seq
         db_events = await event_repo.list_events(
             workflow_id, from_sequence=from_sequence, to_sequence=max_seq
         )
         for event in db_events:
             yield format_sse(event.to_sse_dict(workflow.workflow_id))

         # 4. Consume live events, dropping any with sequence <= max_seq
         async for event in live_stream:
             if event.payload.get("sequence", 0) > max_seq:
                 yield format_sse(event.payload)
     3. Add get_max_sequence() to WorkflowEventRepository:
     async def get_max_sequence(self, workflow_id: UUID) -> int:
         stmt = select(func.coalesce(func.max(WorkflowEvent.sequence), 0)).where(
             WorkflowEvent.workflow_id == workflow_id
         )
         result = await self._session.execute(stmt)
         return result.scalar_one()
     4. Update tests to pass from_sequence=0 (or update endpoint default)

     Files:
     - apps/api/application/event_stream.py (add subscribe_live)
     - apps/api/routes/workflows.py (fix replay logic)
     - apps/api/infrastructure/db/repositories/workflow_event.py (add get_max_sequence)
     - apps/api/tests/e2e/test_gate_e.py (update if needed)

     class ApproveRequest(BaseModel):

     2. Replace _validate_state_version with _validate_optimistic_concurrency:
     def _validate_optimistic_concurrency(
         self,
         workflow: Workflow,
         step_execution: StepExecution,
         expected_state_version: int,
         expected_position: ExpectedPosition,
     ) -> None:
         # Version check
         if expected_state_version != workflow.state_version:
             raise StateVersionMismatchError(
                 expected=expected_state_version,
                 actual=workflow.state_version,
             )

         # Full position check (includes step_name)
         if (expected_position.pass_type != workflow.current_pass.value or
             expected_position.step_name != workflow.current_step.value or
             expected_position.step_number != workflow.current_step_number or
             expected_position.status != step_execution.status.value):

                 message="Position mismatch",
                 expected=expected_position.model_dump(),
                 actual={...},
             )

     Fix 4: Staleness Schema + Trigger Alignment (Issues 4, 5, 6, 7, 17)

     Problem:
     - Artifact model has is_stale, spec requires stale
     - Missing supersedes/superseded_by columns for lineage
     - Trigger uses nonexistent from_step_number (column is from_step)
     - Trigger only marks same-step older revisions, not DOWNSTREAM artifacts per §9.4

     Solution: Align schema with spec. Use exact trigger SQL from spec §7.

     Changes:

     1. Create NEW migration 003_remediation.py:
     -- ============================================================
     -- ARTIFACT LINEAGE COLUMNS (per spec §7)
     -- ============================================================
     ALTER TABLE artifacts
     ADD COLUMN supersedes UUID REFERENCES artifacts(id),
     ADD COLUMN superseded_by UUID REFERENCES artifacts(id);

     -- Rename is_stale to stale (spec uses 'stale')
     ALTER TABLE artifacts RENAME COLUMN is_stale TO stale;

     -- Unique index: exactly one "latest" per lineage (methodology_doc)
     CREATE UNIQUE INDEX idx_latest_methodology_doc ON artifacts(
         workflow_id, step_name, artifact_type, document_type, document_version
     ) WHERE artifact_type = 'methodology_doc' AND superseded_by IS NULL;

     -- Unique index: exactly one "latest" per lineage (step_package)
     CREATE UNIQUE INDEX idx_latest_step_package ON artifacts(
         workflow_id, step_name, artifact_type
     ) WHERE artifact_type = 'step_package' AND superseded_by IS NULL;

     -- Convenience view for latest artifacts
     CREATE VIEW latest_artifacts AS
     SELECT * FROM artifacts WHERE superseded_by IS NULL;

     -- ============================================================
     -- TRACEABILITY LINK STALENESS (per spec §7)
     -- ============================================================
     ALTER TABLE traceability_links
     ADD COLUMN stale BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,
     ADD COLUMN stale_reason VARCHAR(500) NULL,
     ADD COLUMN stale_since TIMESTAMPTZ NULL,
     ADD COLUMN validated_at TIMESTAMPTZ NULL;

     -- ============================================================
     -- STALENESS PROPAGATION TRIGGER (exact spec §7 SQL)
     -- ============================================================
     -- When a new artifact revision is inserted (supersedes IS NOT NULL),
     -- mark DOWNSTREAM latest artifacts and traceability links as stale.

     CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION propagate_staleness()
     RETURNS TRIGGER AS $$
     BEGIN
         -- Only propagate if this is a new revision (not first creation)
         IF NEW.supersedes IS NOT NULL THEN
             -- Mark downstream latest artifacts as stale
             UPDATE artifacts
             SET stale = TRUE,
                 stale_reason = 'upstream_step_' || NEW.step_number || '_revised',
                 stale_since = NOW()
             WHERE workflow_id = NEW.workflow_id
               AND step_number > NEW.step_number  -- DOWNSTREAM steps
               AND superseded_by IS NULL  -- Only mark latest versions
               AND stale = FALSE;

             -- Also mark affected traceability links
             UPDATE traceability_links
             SET stale = TRUE,
                 stale_reason = 'source_step_' || NEW.step_number || '_revised',
                 stale_since = NOW()
             WHERE workflow_id = NEW.workflow_id
               AND from_step = NEW.step_number  -- Links FROM this step
               AND stale = FALSE;
         END IF;
         RETURN NEW;
     END;
     $$ LANGUAGE plpgsql;

     -- Trigger fires on INSERT when supersedes IS NOT NULL (new revision)
     CREATE TRIGGER artifact_staleness_trigger
     AFTER INSERT ON artifacts
     FOR EACH ROW
     WHEN (NEW.supersedes IS NOT NULL)
     EXECUTE FUNCTION propagate_staleness();

     -- ============================================================
     -- ADVISORY LOCK FIX (collision-resistant)
     -- ============================================================
     CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION get_next_event_sequence(p_workflow_id UUID)
     RETURNS INTEGER AS $$
     DECLARE
         next_seq INTEGER;
         lock_id BIGINT;
     BEGIN
         lock_id := hashtext(p_workflow_id::text)::bigint;
         PERFORM pg_advisory_xact_lock(lock_id);
         SELECT COALESCE(MAX(sequence), 0) + 1 INTO next_seq
         FROM workflow_events WHERE workflow_id = p_workflow_id;
         RETURN next_seq;
     END;
     $$ LANGUAGE plpgsql;

     -- ============================================================
     -- STEP EXECUTION UPDATED_AT
     -- ============================================================
     ALTER TABLE step_executions
     ADD COLUMN updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW();

     CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION update_step_execution_timestamp()
     RETURNS TRIGGER AS $$
     BEGIN
         NEW.updated_at = NOW();
         RETURN NEW;
     END;
     $$ LANGUAGE plpgsql;

     CREATE TRIGGER step_execution_updated_at
     BEFORE UPDATE ON step_executions
     FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION update_step_execution_timestamp();
     2. Update Artifact model (artifact.py):
       - Rename is_stale → stale
       - Add supersedes, superseded_by columns
     3. Update TraceabilityLink model (traceability.py):
       - Add stale, stale_reason, stale_since, validated_at columns
     4. Update StalenessResponse schema (routes/workflows.py):

     ---
     Fix 6: Validation Error Handling (Issue 14)

     Problem: Spec requires ValidationErrorResponse (400) for missing fields; FastAPI returns 422.

     Solution: Add custom exception handler that applies to ALL request validation errors across ALL endpoints.

     Changes:

     1. Add global exception handler in main.py:
     from fastapi.exceptions import RequestValidationError
     from starlette.responses import JSONResponse

     class ValidationErrorResponse(BaseModel):
         """400 response for request validation failures."""
         error_code: str = "VALIDATION_ERROR"
         message: str
         errors: list[dict]

     @app.exception_handler(RequestValidationError)
     async def validation_exception_handler(request: Request, exc: RequestValidationError):
         """Convert FastAPI 422 to spec-compliant 400 ValidationErrorResponse.

         Applies to ALL endpoints, not just action endpoints.
         """
         return JSONResponse(
             status_code=400,
             content=ValidationErrorResponse(
                 message="Request validation failed",
                 errors=[{"loc": list(e["loc"]), "msg": e["msg"], "type": e["type"]} for e in exc.errors()],
             ).model_dump(),
         )

     Note: This handler applies globally to all endpoints (workflows, messages, progress, etc.), ensuring spec 
     compliance throughout.

     ---
     Fix 7: state_version Increment Rule (Issue 13)

     Problem: state_version rules vague; need concrete before/after comparison. Must include status/phase 
     changes EVEN when position doesn't change.

     Solution: Use explicit before/after comparisons. Increment on ANY state-eligibility change.

     Changes:

     1. Update sync_db_from_state (workflow_service.py):
     # Capture state BEFORE changes
     old_pass = workflow.current_pass
     old_step = workflow.current_step
     old_step_number = workflow.current_step_number
     old_status = step_execution.status if step_execution else None
     old_phase = step_execution.phase if step_execution else None

     # ... apply state changes ...

     # Detect state-eligibility changes (Contract §11.3)
     # IMPORTANT: status/phase changes increment EVEN if position unchanged
     state_eligibility_changed = (
         # Position changes
         old_pass != workflow.current_pass or
         old_step != workflow.current_step or
         old_step_number != workflow.current_step_number or
         # Status changes (EVEN without position change)
         (step_execution and old_status != step_execution.status) or
         # Phase changes (EVEN without position change)
         (step_execution and old_phase != step_execution.phase) or
         # New artifacts created
         len(artifact_results) > 0
     )

     if state_eligibility_changed:
         await self._increment_state_version(workflow)
     2. Action methods always increment (they change status/phase):
       - approve_step(): status changes (awaiting_review → approved) → increment
       - revise_step(): status changes + new artifact → increment
       - clarify_step(): phase changes → increment
     3. Explicit increment scenarios:
     | Change                             | Position Changed? | Increment? |
     |------------------------------------|-------------------|------------|
     | Step advances                      | Yes               | Yes        |
     | Status: pending → running          | No                | Yes        |
     | Status: awaiting_review → approved | No                | Yes        |
     | Phase: received → drafting         | No                | Yes        |
     | New artifact created               | No                | Yes        |
     | Staleness updated                  | No                | Yes        |

     step.awaiting_review)
     - ALL events published AFTER transaction commits (route does publish loop)
     - SSE payloads include event_type, event_id (=workflow_events.id), sequence, external workflow_id
